Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: I hear a woman’s voice in the hallway or through the air vent saying things like, “You’re going to lose it all,” and it sounds so real that I’ve gone outside to see who was there.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: My sister will tell me something funny or upsetting and I know I should react, but my face feels blank and it takes effort to answer with more than a few words.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to plan brunches, paint in the spare room, go hiking, and sign up for networking events, but now those things feel like tasks I’m pretending to care about.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: My appetite has been all over the place, but mostly I skip real meals during the day and then realize my clothes are looser and I’ve been living on coffee, toast, and whatever is easiest.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: Most nights I lie awake for hours listening to the house settle, then wake up around three or four and can’t get back to sleep before work.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: At work I move so slowly some mornings that opening spreadsheets, typing responses, or walking to a meeting feels like I’m pushing through wet cement.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I came in because I can’t stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault, like I’m a bad employee and a bad homeowner who deserves to lose what I worked for.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the same months I was barely eating, sleeping badly, and feeling like a failure, I was also hearing the woman’s voice and believing people were watching for mistakes with the house and my job.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the low mood and loss of interest have been there for almost the whole time since this started, with only brief periods where I could function a little It makes me feel like I’m a bad employee and a bad homeowner, and like maybe I deserve to lose what I worked for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most nights I’m lying awake for hours, just listening to the house settling and noises like that. And then I’ll wake up around three or four in the morning and I can’t really get back to sleep before I have to go to work.\n\nDoctor: Even when you're not dealing with townhouse-related problems, do you still feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when nothing specific is happening with the townhouse, I still feel down. Looking back, it’s been there most of the time since all this started. I might have short stretches where I can manage a little better, but the low mood and not really enjoying things don’t really go away.\n\nDoctor: During this time, was there ever a period of at least two weeks when your depression was especially severe most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been pretty constant, honestly. The depression and not being interested in things have been with me for about two years and one month, and there were definitely stretches in there where it felt really severe most of the day, almost every day.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-year period, had you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember having another period before this that was like that for two weeks straight, but it’s hard to say for certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than your normal self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I had more energy than usual, but not like unusually high or way out of character for me. At least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry excessively about many different areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say excessively. I worry about things sometimes, like normal life stuff, but I haven’t really noticed it being about lots of different areas all the time.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had a poor appetite or been overeating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my appetite’s been pretty inconsistent. Most days I don’t really eat proper meals during the day. I’ll have coffee, maybe toast, or just whatever’s easiest, and then I notice my clothes are fitting looser.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I keep going over everything I did wrong, or what I should’ve done, and it turns into this feeling that it’s all my fault—like I’m just failing at work and at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different parts of your life, even when there isn’t a clear reason to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. It feels like I worry a lot, but I don’t know if it’s about everything. It mostly seems to land on whatever I think I’m messing up, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I’ve been feeling depressed, and kind of losing interest in things, for a long time now — about two years, maybe a little over. Around two years and a month.\n\nDoctor: During the past two years, was there ever a stretch of two months or longer when you were not feeling depressed or uninterested in things?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Looking back, it’s been there pretty much the whole time. There were maybe short times where I could get through things a little better, but not like two months where I felt okay or really interested in things again.\n\nDoctor: Did these depressed feelings start after a specific stressful event or major change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it did start after a specific change. It was after I bought my townhouse. At first it seemed like a good step, but then the pressure of keeping up with it just started building. Since then, it’s been going on for about two years and three months.\n\nDoctor: Do your depressed feelings mainly center on the stress of owning and keeping up with the townhouse?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly. It started after I bought the townhouse, and the pressure of trying to keep up with everything just kept building. It’s been going on about two years and three months now.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you often felt worthless or had very low self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel worthless a lot. I keep thinking everything that goes wrong is my fault, like I’m failing at work and failing with the townhouse. It makes me feel like I’m a bad employee and a bad homeowner, and like maybe I deserve to lose what I worked for.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most nights I’m lying awake for hours, just listening to the house settling and noises like that. And then I’ll wake up around three or four in the morning and I can’t really get back to sleep before I have to go to work.\n\nDoctor: Even when you're not dealing with townhouse-related problems, do you still feel depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. Even when nothing specific is happening with the townhouse, I still feel down. Looking back, it’s been there most of the time since all this started. I might have short stretches where I can manage a little better, but the low mood and not really enjoying things don’t really go away.\n\nDoctor: During this time, was there ever a period of at least two weeks when your depression was especially severe most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been pretty constant, honestly. The depression and not being interested in things have been with me for about two years and one month, and there were definitely stretches in there where it felt really severe most of the day, almost every day.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-year period, had you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t remember having another period before this that was like that for two weeks straight, but it’s hard to say for certain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually energetic or high, much more than your normal self?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I had more energy than usual, but not like unusually high or way out of character for me. At least not that I can clearly point to.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry excessively about many different areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say excessively. I worry about things sometimes, like normal life stuff, but I haven’t really noticed it being about lots of different areas all the time.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you had a poor appetite or been overeating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my appetite’s been pretty inconsistent. Most days I don’t really eat proper meals during the day. I’ll have coffee, maybe toast, or just whatever’s easiest, and then I notice my clothes are fitting looser.\n\nDoctor: During those times when the depression felt especially severe, did you have trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I don’t remember it standing out as a big issue. When I feel really low, it’s harder to get things done, but I can’t say for sure if it was specifically trouble concentrating.\n\nDoctor: Before this current two-year period, had you ever gone through another episode of depression that lasted at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had periods where I felt down before, but I don’t know if any of them lasted a full two weeks or clearly counted as an episode before this.\n\nDoctor: During this current two-year period, did you ever have thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really have a clear answer for that… I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Before this began, was there ever a time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Before this, I know I had times where I felt down or kind of disconnected from things, but I can’t really say it was nearly every day for two weeks. I don’t have a clear memory of that.\n\nDoctor: During this two-year period, was there ever a time when you felt hopeless nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve felt really low at times, but I don’t know if I’d say hopeless nearly every day. I’d have to think about that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
